What are states neither produced by past action nor subject to clinging? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states neither produced by past action nor subject to clinging. ^1-996

What are states that are defiled and subject to defilement? The three unwholesome roots – greed, hate, delusion; and the defilements that occur together with them; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness; the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states that are defiled and subject to defilement. ^1-997

What are states that are undefiled but subject to defilement? Wholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form, the aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are undefiled but subject to defilement. ^1-998

What are states that are undefiled and not subject to defilement? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states that are undefiled and not subject to defilement. ^1-999

What are states with applied thought and sustained thought? In the plane with applied thought and sustained thought, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or not included in the spheres of existence, except applied thought and sustained thought; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness – these are states with applied thought and sustained thought. ^1-1000

What are states without applied thought but with sustained thought only? In the plane without applied thought but with sustained thought only, whether of the fine-material sphere or not included in the spheres of existence, except sustained thought; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness – these are states without applied thought but with sustained thought only. ^1-1001

What are states without applied thought and without sustained thought? In the plane without applied thought and without sustained thought, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states without applied thought and without sustained thought. ^1-1002

What are states accompanied by rapture? In the plane of rapture, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or not included in the spheres of existence, except rapture; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness – these are states accompanied by rapture. ^1-1003

What are states accompanied by happiness? In the plane of happiness, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or not included in the spheres of existence, except happiness; the associated aggregate of perception, aggregate of mental formations, aggregate of consciousness – these are states accompanied by happiness. ^1-1004

What are states accompanied by equanimity? In the plane of equanimity, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence, except equanimity; the associated aggregate of perception, aggregate of mental formations, aggregate of consciousness – these are states accompanied by equanimity. ^1-1005

What are states to be abandoned by seeing? The three fetters – identity view, doubt, misapprehension of rules and observances. ^1-1006

Therein, what is identity view? Here an uninstructed ordinary person, who does not see the noble ones, is unskilled in the teaching of the noble ones and undisciplined in the teaching of the noble ones, who does not see good people, is unskilled in the teaching of good people and undisciplined in the teaching of good people, regards material form as self, or self as possessing material form, or material form as in self, or self as in material form. He regards feeling as self, or self as possessing feeling, or feeling as in self, or self as in feeling. He regards perception as self, or self as possessing perception, or perception as in self, or self as in perception. He regards mental formations as self, or self as possessing mental formations, or mental formations as in self, or self as in mental formations. He regards consciousness as self, or self as possessing consciousness, or consciousness as in self, or self as in consciousness. Whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views [variant: diṭṭhivisūkāyitaṃ (Sī.)], the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called identity view. ^1-1007

Therein, what is doubt? One is uncertain and in doubt about the Teacher, uncertain and in doubt about the Teaching, uncertain and in doubt about the Community, uncertain and in doubt about the training, uncertain and in doubt about the past, uncertain and in doubt about the future, uncertain and in doubt about both the past and the future, uncertain and in doubt about specific conditionality and dependently arisen states. Whatever uncertainty of this kind there is, being uncertain, the state of being uncertain, perplexity, doubt, indecision, a forked path, wavering, failure to take a definite stand, hesitation, faltering, failure to fathom, stiffness of mind, mental scratching – this is called doubt. ^1-1008

Therein, what is misapprehension of rules and observances? Ascetics and brahmins outside this Teaching hold that "Purification comes through rules, purification comes through observances, purification comes through rules and observances"; whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views, the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called misapprehension of rules and observances. ^1-1009

These three fetters; and the defilements that occur together with them; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness; the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1010

What are states to be abandoned by development? The remaining greed, hate, delusion; and the defilements that occur together with them; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness; the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states to be abandoned by development. ^1-1011

What are states to be abandoned neither by seeing nor by development? Wholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states to be abandoned neither by seeing nor by development. ^1-1012

What are states whose roots are to be abandoned by seeing? The three fetters – identity view, doubt, misapprehension of rules and observances. ^1-1013

Therein, what is identity view… this is called identity view. ^1-1014

Therein, what is doubt… this is called doubt. ^1-1015

Therein, what is misapprehension of rules and observances… this is called misapprehension of rules and observances. ^1-1016

These three fetters; and the defilements that occur together with them; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness; the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states whose roots are to be abandoned by seeing. The three fetters – identity view, doubt, misapprehension of rules and observances – these are states to be abandoned by seeing. The greed, hate, delusion that occur together with them – these are roots to be abandoned by seeing. And the defilements that occur together with them; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness; the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states whose roots are to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1017

What are states whose roots are to be abandoned by development? The remaining greed, hate, delusion – these are roots to be abandoned by development. And the defilements that occur together with them; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness; the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states whose roots are to be abandoned by development. ^1-1018

What are states whose roots are to be abandoned neither by seeing nor by development? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states whose roots are to be abandoned neither by seeing nor by development. ^1-1019

What are states leading to accumulation? Wholesome and unwholesome states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states leading to accumulation. ^1-1020

What are states leading away from accumulation? The four paths not included in the spheres of existence – these are states leading away from accumulation. ^1-1021

What are states leading neither to nor away from accumulation? The results of wholesome and unwholesome states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; and those states that are functional, neither wholesome nor unwholesome nor results of action; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states leading neither to nor away from accumulation. ^1-1022

What are states of one in training? The four paths not included in the spheres of existence, and the three lower fruits of the contemplative life – these are states of one in training. ^1-1023

What are states of one beyond training? The highest [variant: uparimaṃ (Syā.)] fruit, the fruit of arahantship – these are states of one beyond training. ^1-1024

What are states of one neither in training nor beyond training? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states of one neither in training nor beyond training. ^1-1025

What are limited states? All wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states of the sense sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are limited states. ^1-1026

What are exalted states? Wholesome and indeterminate states of the fine-material sphere and of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are exalted states. ^1-1027

What are immeasurable states? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are immeasurable states. ^1-1028

What are states with a limited object? The mind and mental factors that arise taking limited states as their object – these are states with a limited object. ^1-1029

What are states with an exalted object? The mind and mental factors that arise taking exalted states as their object – these are states with an exalted object. ^1-1030

What are states with an immeasurable object? The mind and mental factors that arise taking immeasurable states as their object – these are states with an immeasurable object. ^1-1031

What are inferior states? The three unwholesome roots – greed, hate, delusion; and the defilements that occur together with them; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness; the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are inferior states. ^1-1032

What are middling states? Wholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere, the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are middling states. ^1-1033

What are superior states? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are superior states. ^1-1034

What are states fixed in wrongness? The five actions with immediate result, and the wrong views that are fixed – these are states fixed in wrongness. ^1-1035

What are states fixed in rightness? The four paths not included in the spheres of existence – these are states fixed in rightness. ^1-1036

What are states that are not fixed? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not fixed. ^1-1037

What are states with the path as their object? The mind and mental factors that arise taking the noble path as their object – these are states with the path as their object. ^1-1038

What are states with the path as their root? Except the path factors in one who possesses the noble path; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness – these are states with the path as their root. In one who possesses the noble path, right view is both path and root; except right view, the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness – these are states with the path as their root. In one who possesses the noble path, non-greed, non-hate, non-delusion – these are roots that belong to the path. The associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness – these are states with the path as their root. ^1-1039

What are states with the path as their dominant factor? The mind and mental factors that arise making the noble path their dominant factor – these are states with the path as their dominant factor. In one who possesses the noble path and develops the path with inquiry as the dominant factor, except inquiry; the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness – these are states with the path as their dominant factor. ^1-1040

What are arisen states? Those states that are born, come to be, produced, generated, fully generated, made manifest, arisen, fully arisen, risen, fully risen, arisen, and included under the heading "arisen": material form [variant: rūpā (in many editions)], feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness – these are arisen states. ^1-1041

What are states that have not arisen? Those states that are not born, not come to be, not produced, not generated, not fully generated, not made manifest, not arisen, not fully arisen, not risen, not fully risen, not arisen, and included under the heading "not arisen": material form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness – these are states that have not arisen. ^1-1042

What are states that are bound to arise? The results of wholesome and unwholesome states whose results have not yet ripened, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; and whatever material form will arise due to past action – these are states that are bound to arise. ^1-1043

What are past states? Those states that are past, ceased, gone, changed, ended, completely ended, gone after having arisen, past, and included under the heading "past": material form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness – these are past states. ^1-1044

What are future states? Those states that are not born, not come to be, not produced, not generated, not fully generated, not made manifest, not arisen, not fully arisen, not risen, not fully risen, future, and included under the heading "future": material form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness – these are future states. ^1-1045

What are present states? Those states that are born, come to be, produced, generated, fully generated, made manifest, arisen, fully arisen, risen, fully risen, present, and included under the heading "present": material form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness – these are present states. ^1-1046

What are states with a past object? The mind and mental factors that arise taking past states as their object – these are states with a past object. ^1-1047

What are states with a future object? The mind and mental factors that arise taking future states as their object – these are states with a future object. ^1-1048

What are states with a present object? The mind and mental factors that arise taking present states as their object – these are states with a present object. ^1-1049

What are one's own states? Those states that are within each particular being, personal to it, bound to it, individual to it and produced by past action: material form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness – these are one's own states. ^1-1050

What are others' states? Those states that are within each particular other being and other person, personal to them, bound to them, individual to them and produced by past action: material form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness – these are others' states. ^1-1051

What are states both one's own and others'? Both of these – these are states both one's own and others'. ^1-1052

What are states with one's own states as object? The mind and mental factors that arise taking one's own states as their object – these are states with one's own states as object. ^1-1053

What are states with others' states as object? The mind and mental factors that arise taking others' states as their object – these are states with others' states as object. ^1-1054

What are states with both one's own and others' states as object? The mind and mental factors that arise taking states both one's own and others' as their object – these are states with both one's own and others' states as object. ^1-1055

What are states that are visible and make impact? The visible-form base – these are states that are visible and make impact. ^1-1056

What are states that are invisible but make impact? The eye base, the ear base, the nose base, the tongue base, the body base, the sound base, the smell base, the taste base, the touch base – these are states that are invisible but make impact. ^1-1057

What are states that are invisible and make no impact? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, the aggregate of consciousness; and whatever material form is invisible, makes no impact and is included in the mental-object base; and the unconditioned element – these are states that are invisible and make no impact.
The sets of three. ^1-1058

#### Summary of the Pairs ^1-3-2-0

##### The Group on Roots ^1-3-2-1-0

What are states that are roots? Three wholesome roots, three unwholesome roots, three indeterminate roots, nine roots of the sense sphere, six roots of the fine-material sphere, six roots of the immaterial sphere, six roots not included in the spheres of existence. ^1-1059

Therein, what are the three wholesome roots? Non-greed, non-hate, non-delusion. ^1-1060

Therein, what is non-greed? Whatever is non-greed, not being greedy, the state of not being greedy, non-lust, not lusting, the state of not lusting, non-covetousness, non-greed, the wholesome root – this is called non-greed. ^1-1061

Therein, what is non-hate? Whatever is non-hate, not hating, the state of not hating, loving-kindness, being loving, the state of being loving, kindness, being kind, the state of being kind, seeking others' welfare, sympathy, non-ill-will, absence of malice, non-hate, the wholesome root – this is called non-hate. ^1-1062

Therein, what is non-delusion? Knowledge of suffering, knowledge of the arising of suffering, knowledge of the cessation of suffering, knowledge of the way leading to the cessation of suffering, knowledge of the past, knowledge of the future, knowledge of both the past and the future, knowledge of specific conditionality and dependently arisen states, whatever wisdom of this kind there is, understanding, investigation, close investigation, investigation of states, discerning, distinguishing, differentiating, learning, skill, subtlety, clarity, consideration, examination, breadth, intelligence, guidance, insight, clear awareness, a goad, wisdom, the faculty of wisdom, the power of wisdom, the sword of wisdom, the tower of wisdom, the light of wisdom, the radiance of wisdom, the lamp of wisdom, the jewel of wisdom, non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called non-delusion.
These are the three wholesome roots. ^1-1063

Therein, what are the three unwholesome roots? Greed, hate, delusion. ^1-1064

Therein, what is greed? Whatever is lust, passion, compliance, accommodation, delight, delight and lust [variant: nandirāgo (Sī.)], passion of mind, wanting, infatuation, holding on, eagerness, intense eagerness, attachment, the mire, agitation, deceit, the begetter, the producer, the seamstress [variant: sibbanī (Sī.)], the net-maker, the stream, the entangler, the thread, the spreading one, the accumulator [variant: āyūhanī (Sī., Syā.)], the companion, aspiration, the conduit to existence, the forest, the undergrowth, intimacy, affection, concern, kinship, hope, hoping, the state of hoping [variant: āsiṃsanā āsiṃsitattaṃ (Sī., Syā.)], hope for visible forms, hope for sounds, hope for smells, hope for tastes, hope for touches, hope for gain, hope for wealth, hope for children, hope for life, hankering, intense hankering, extreme hankering, hankering, the act of hankering, the state of hankering, voracity, being voracious, the state of voracity, fawning [variant: puñcikatā (Syā.) pucchikatā (Sī.)], the wish for fine things, unrighteous lust, lawless greed, liking, desiring, longing, envying, earnest longing, sensual craving, craving for continued existence, craving for non-existence, craving for fine-material existence, craving for immaterial existence, craving for cessation, craving for visible forms, craving for sounds, craving for smells, craving for tastes, craving for touches, craving for mental objects, the flood, the yoke, the tie, clinging, the obstruction, the hindrance, the covering, the bond, the corruption, the underlying tendency, the obsession, the creeper, meanness, the root of suffering, the source of suffering, the origin of suffering, Māra's snare, Māra's hook, Māra's domain, the river of craving, the net of craving, the leash of craving, the ocean of craving, covetousness, greed, the unwholesome root – this is called greed. ^1-1065

Therein, what is hate? Annoyance arises at the thought "He has done me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He is doing me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He will do me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He has done harm to someone dear and pleasing to me… he is doing harm… he will do harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He has done good to someone not dear and not pleasing to me… he is doing good… he will do good", or annoyance arises without reason. Whatever annoyance of mind of this kind there is, resentment, aversion, antagonism, irritation, exasperation, fury, hate, hatred, intense hatred, malice of mind, mental hatred, anger, being angry, the state of being angry, hate, hating, the state of hating, malice, being malicious, the state of being malicious, hostility, antagonism, fierceness, abusiveness, discontent of mind – this is called hate. ^1-1066

Therein, what is delusion? Lack of knowledge of suffering, lack of knowledge of the arising of suffering, lack of knowledge of the cessation of suffering, lack of knowledge of the way leading to the cessation of suffering, lack of knowledge of the past, lack of knowledge of the future, lack of knowledge of both the past and the future, lack of knowledge of specific conditionality and dependently arisen states, whatever lack of knowledge of this kind there is, lack of vision, non-realisation, lack of understanding, lack of awakening, non-penetration, failure to take in, failure to fathom, failure to consider, failure to review, failure to see for oneself, stupidity, foolishness, lack of clear awareness, delusion, deep delusion, utter delusion, ignorance, the flood of ignorance, the yoke of ignorance, the underlying tendency to ignorance, the obsession of ignorance, the bar of ignorance, delusion, the unwholesome root – this is called delusion.
These are the three unwholesome roots. ^1-1067

Therein, what are the three indeterminate roots? Non-greed, non-hate and non-delusion arising as results of wholesome states, or in indeterminate states that are functional – these are the three indeterminate roots. ^1-1068

Therein, what are the nine roots of the sense sphere? The three wholesome roots, the three unwholesome roots, the three indeterminate roots – these are the nine roots of the sense sphere. ^1-1069

Therein, what are the six roots of the fine-material sphere? The three wholesome roots, the three indeterminate roots – these are the six roots of the fine-material sphere. ^1-1070

Therein, what are the six roots of the immaterial sphere? The three wholesome roots, the three indeterminate roots – these are the six roots of the immaterial sphere. ^1-1071

Therein, what are the six roots not included in the spheres of existence? The three wholesome roots, the three indeterminate roots – these are the six roots not included in the spheres of existence. ^1-1072

Therein, what are the three wholesome roots? Non-greed, non-hate, non-delusion. ^1-1073

Therein, what is non-greed? Whatever is non-greed, not being greedy, the state of not being greedy, non-lust, not lusting, the state of not lusting, non-covetousness, non-greed, the wholesome root – this is called non-greed. ^1-1074

Therein, what is non-hate? Whatever is non-hate, not hating, the state of not hating… non-ill-will, absence of malice, non-hate, the wholesome root – this is called non-hate. ^1-1075

Therein, what is non-delusion? Knowledge of suffering, knowledge of the arising of suffering, knowledge of the cessation of suffering, knowledge of the way leading to the cessation of suffering, knowledge of the past, knowledge of the future, knowledge of both the past and the future, knowledge of specific conditionality and dependently arisen states, whatever wisdom of this kind there is, understanding, investigation, close investigation, investigation of states, discerning, distinguishing, differentiating, learning, skill, subtlety, clarity, consideration, examination, breadth, intelligence, guidance, insight, clear awareness, a goad, wisdom, the faculty of wisdom, the power of wisdom, the sword of wisdom, the tower of wisdom, the light of wisdom, the radiance of wisdom, the lamp of wisdom, the jewel of wisdom, non-delusion, investigation of states, right view, investigation of states as a factor of awakening, a path factor, included in the path – this is called non-delusion.
These are the three wholesome roots. ^1-1076

Therein, what are the three indeterminate roots? Non-greed, non-hate and non-delusion arising as results of wholesome states – these are the three indeterminate roots. These are the six roots not included in the spheres of existence – these are states that are roots. ^1-1077

What are states that are not roots? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not roots. ^1-1078

What are states with roots? The states that, in relation to those states, have roots: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states with roots. ^1-1079

What are states without roots? The states that, in relation to those states, are without roots: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states without roots. ^1-1080

What are states associated with roots? The states that are associated with those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states associated with roots. ^1-1081

What are states dissociated from roots? The states that are dissociated from those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from roots. ^1-1082

What are states that are both roots and have roots? Greed is both a root and has a root through delusion, delusion is both a root and has a root through greed, hate is both a root and has a root through delusion, delusion is both a root and has a root through hate; non-greed, non-hate, non-delusion are each both roots and have roots through one another – these are states that are both roots and have roots. ^1-1083

What are states that have roots but are not roots? Among the states that, in relation to those states, have roots, except those states, the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that have roots but are not roots. ^1-1084

What are states that are both roots and associated with roots? Greed is both a root and associated with a root through delusion, delusion is both a root and associated with a root through greed, hate is both a root and associated with a root through delusion, delusion is both a root and associated with a root through hate; non-greed, non-hate, non-delusion are each both roots and associated with roots through one another – these are states that are both roots and associated with roots. ^1-1085

What are states that are associated with roots but are not roots? Among the states that are associated with those states, except those states, the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are associated with roots but are not roots. ^1-1086

What are states that are not roots but have roots? The states that, in relation to those states, are not roots but have roots: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are not roots but have roots. ^1-1087

What are states that are not roots and are without roots? The states that, in relation to those states, are not roots and are without roots: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not roots and are without roots. ^1-1088

##### The Short Intermediate Pairs ^1-3-2-2-0

What are states that have conditions? The five aggregates – the aggregate of material form, the aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that have conditions. ^1-1089

What are states without conditions? The unconditioned element – these are states without conditions. ^1-1090

What are conditioned states? Whatever states have conditions, those very states are conditioned. ^1-1091

What are unconditioned states? Whatever state is without conditions, that very state is unconditioned. ^1-1092

What are visible states? The visible-form base – these are visible states. ^1-1093

What are invisible states? The eye base… the touch base, the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness, whatever material form is invisible, makes no impact and is included in the mental-object base, and the unconditioned element – these are invisible states. ^1-1094

What are states that make impact? The eye base… the touch base – these are states that make impact. ^1-1095

What are states that make no impact? The aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness, whatever material form is invisible, makes no impact and is included in the mental-object base, and the unconditioned element – these are states that make no impact. ^1-1096

What are material states? The four great elements and the material form derived from the four great elements – these are material states. ^1-1097

What are immaterial states? The aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness, and the unconditioned element – these are immaterial states. ^1-1098

What are worldly states? Wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere, the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are worldly states. ^1-1099

What are states that transcend the world? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states that transcend the world. ^1-1100

What are states that some kinds of consciousness can know and states that some kinds of consciousness cannot know? The states that eye-consciousness can know are not states that ear-consciousness can know; and the states that ear-consciousness can know are not states that eye-consciousness can know. The states that eye-consciousness can know are not states that nose-consciousness can know; and the states that nose-consciousness can know are not states that eye-consciousness can know. The states that eye-consciousness can know are not states that tongue-consciousness can know; and the states that tongue-consciousness can know are not states that eye-consciousness can know. The states that eye-consciousness can know are not states that body-consciousness can know; and the states that body-consciousness can know are not states that eye-consciousness can know. The states that ear-consciousness can know are not states that nose-consciousness can know; and the states that nose-consciousness can know are not states that ear-consciousness can know. The states that ear-consciousness can know are not states that tongue-consciousness can know; and the states that tongue-consciousness can know are not states that ear-consciousness can know. The states that ear-consciousness can know are not states that body-consciousness can know; and the states that body-consciousness can know are not states that ear-consciousness can know. The states that ear-consciousness can know are not states that eye-consciousness can know; and the states that eye-consciousness can know are not states that ear-consciousness can know. The states that nose-consciousness can know are not states that tongue-consciousness can know; and the states that tongue-consciousness can know are not states that nose-consciousness can know. The states that nose-consciousness can know are not states that body-consciousness can know; and the states that body-consciousness can know are not states that nose-consciousness can know. The states that nose-consciousness can know are not states that eye-consciousness can know; and the states that eye-consciousness can know are not states that nose-consciousness can know. The states that nose-consciousness can know are not states that ear-consciousness can know; and the states that ear-consciousness can know are not states that nose-consciousness can know. The states that tongue-consciousness can know are not states that body-consciousness can know; and the states that body-consciousness can know are not states that tongue-consciousness can know. The states that tongue-consciousness can know are not states that eye-consciousness can know; and the states that eye-consciousness can know are not states that tongue-consciousness can know. The states that tongue-consciousness can know are not states that ear-consciousness can know; and the states that ear-consciousness can know are not states that tongue-consciousness can know. The states that tongue-consciousness can know are not states that nose-consciousness can know; and the states that nose-consciousness can know are not states that tongue-consciousness can know. The states that body-consciousness can know are not states that eye-consciousness can know; and the states that eye-consciousness can know are not states that body-consciousness can know. The states that body-consciousness can know are not states that ear-consciousness can know; and the states that ear-consciousness can know are not states that body-consciousness can know. The states that body-consciousness can know are not states that nose-consciousness can know; and the states that nose-consciousness can know are not states that body-consciousness can know. The states that body-consciousness can know are not states that tongue-consciousness can know; and the states that tongue-consciousness can know are not states that body-consciousness can know. These are states that some kinds of consciousness can know and states that some kinds of consciousness cannot know. ^1-1101

##### The Group on Taints ^1-3-2-3-0

What are states that are taints? The four taints – the taint of sensual desire, the taint of existence, the taint of views, the taint of ignorance. ^1-1102

Therein, what is the taint of sensual desire? Whatever, with regard to sensual pleasures, is sensual desire, sensual lust, sensual delight, sensual craving, sensual affection, sensual fever, sensual infatuation, holding on to sensual pleasures – this is called the taint of sensual desire. ^1-1103

Therein, what is the taint of existence? Whatever, with regard to states of existence, is desire for existence [variant: bhavacchando (Sī., Syā.)], lust for existence, delight in existence, craving for continued existence, affection for existence, fever for existence, infatuation with existence, holding on to existence – this is called the taint of existence. ^1-1104

Therein, what is the taint of views? "The world is eternal" or "The world is not eternal" or "The world has an end" or "The world has no end" or "The soul and the body are the same" or "The soul is one thing and the body another" or "The Tathāgata exists after death" or "The Tathāgata does not exist after death" or "The Tathāgata both exists and does not exist after death" or "The Tathāgata neither exists nor does not exist after death"; whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views, the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called the taint of views. All wrong view is the taint of views. ^1-1105

Therein, what is the taint of ignorance? Lack of knowledge of suffering, lack of knowledge of the arising of suffering, lack of knowledge of the cessation of suffering, lack of knowledge of the way leading to the cessation of suffering, lack of knowledge of the past, lack of knowledge of the future, lack of knowledge of both the past and the future, lack of knowledge of specific conditionality and dependently arisen states; whatever lack of knowledge of this kind there is, lack of vision, non-realisation, lack of understanding, lack of awakening, non-penetration, failure to take in, failure to fathom, failure to consider, failure to review, failure to see for oneself, stupidity, foolishness, lack of clear awareness, delusion, deep delusion, utter delusion, ignorance, the flood of ignorance, the yoke of ignorance, the underlying tendency to ignorance, the obsession of ignorance, the bar of ignorance, delusion, the unwholesome root – this is called the taint of ignorance.
These states are taints. ^1-1106

What are states that are not taints? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not taints. ^1-1107

What are states subject to taints? Wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states subject to taints. ^1-1108

What are states free of taints? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states free of taints. ^1-1109

What are states associated with taints? The states that are associated with those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states associated with taints. ^1-1110

What are states dissociated from taints? The states that are dissociated from those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from taints. ^1-1111

What are states that are both taints and subject to taints? Those same taints are states that are both taints and subject to taints. ^1-1112

What are states that are subject to taints but are not taints? Among the states that, in relation to those states, are subject to taints, except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are subject to taints but are not taints. ^1-1113

What are states that are both taints and associated with taints? The taint of sensual desire is both a taint and associated with a taint through the taint of ignorance, the taint of ignorance is both a taint and associated with a taint through the taint of sensual desire, the taint of existence is both a taint and associated with a taint through the taint of ignorance, the taint of ignorance is both a taint and associated with a taint through the taint of existence, the taint of views is both a taint and associated with a taint through the taint of ignorance, the taint of ignorance is both a taint and associated with a taint through the taint of views – these are states that are both taints and associated with taints. ^1-1114

What are states that are associated with taints but are not taints? Among the states that are associated with those states, except those states, the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are associated with taints but are not taints. ^1-1115

What are states dissociated from taints but subject to taints? The states that are dissociated from those states and are wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere, the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states dissociated from taints but subject to taints. ^1-1116

What are states dissociated from taints and free of taints? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from taints and free of taints.
The first recitation section of the chapter of summaries. ^1-1117

##### The Group on Fetters ^1-3-2-4-0

What are states that are fetters? The ten fetters – the fetter of sensual lust, the fetter of aversion, the fetter of conceit, the fetter of views, the fetter of doubt, the fetter of misapprehension of rules and observances, the fetter of lust for existence, the fetter of envy, the fetter of stinginess, the fetter of ignorance. ^1-1118

Therein, what is the fetter of sensual lust? Whatever, with regard to sensual pleasures, is sensual desire, sensual lust, sensual delight, sensual craving, sensual affection, sensual fever, sensual infatuation, holding on to sensual pleasures – this is called the fetter of sensual lust. ^1-1119

Therein, what is the fetter of aversion? Annoyance arises at the thought "He has done me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He is doing me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He will do me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He has done harm to someone dear and pleasing to me… he is doing harm… he will do harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He has done good to someone not dear and not pleasing to me… he is doing good… he will do good", or annoyance arises without reason. Whatever annoyance of mind of this kind there is, resentment, aversion, antagonism, irritation, exasperation, fury, hate, hatred, intense hatred, malice of mind, mental hatred, anger, being angry, the state of being angry, hate, hating, the state of hating, malice, being malicious, the state of being malicious, hostility, antagonism, fierceness, abusiveness, discontent of mind – this is called the fetter of aversion. ^1-1120

Therein, what is the fetter of conceit? Conceit that "I am better", conceit that "I am equal", conceit that "I am inferior". Whatever conceit of this kind there is, conceiving, the state of having conceived, loftiness, haughtiness [variant: uṇṇati uṇṇāmo (Syā.)], flag-raising, self-exaltation, the mind's wish to fly its banner – this is called the fetter of conceit. ^1-1121

Therein, what is the fetter of views? "The world is eternal" or "The world is not eternal" or "The world has an end" or "The world has no end" or "The soul and the body are the same" or "The soul is one thing and the body another" or "The Tathāgata exists after death" or "The Tathāgata does not exist after death" or "The Tathāgata both exists and does not exist after death" or "The Tathāgata neither exists nor does not exist after death"; whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views, the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called the fetter of views. Except the fetter of misapprehension of rules and observances, all wrong view is the fetter of views. ^1-1122

Therein, what is the fetter of doubt? One is uncertain and in doubt about the Teacher, uncertain and in doubt about the Teaching, uncertain and in doubt about the Community, uncertain and in doubt about the training, uncertain and in doubt about the past, uncertain and in doubt about the future, uncertain and in doubt about both the past and the future, uncertain and in doubt about specific conditionality and dependently arisen states; whatever uncertainty of this kind there is, being uncertain, the state of being uncertain, perplexity, doubt, indecision, a forked path, wavering, failure to take a definite stand, hesitation, faltering, failure to fathom, stiffness of mind, mental scratching – this is called the fetter of doubt. ^1-1123

Therein, what is the fetter of misapprehension of rules and observances? Ascetics and brahmins outside this Teaching hold that "Purification comes through rules, purification comes through observances, purification comes through rules and observances"; whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views, the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called the fetter of misapprehension of rules and observances. ^1-1124

Therein, what is the fetter of lust for existence? Whatever, with regard to states of existence, is desire for existence, lust for existence, delight in existence, craving for continued existence, affection for existence, fever for existence, infatuation with existence, holding on to existence – this is called the fetter of lust for existence. ^1-1125

Therein, what is the fetter of envy? Whatever envy there is at others' gain, honour, respect, reverence, salutation and veneration, envying, the state of envying, jealousy, being jealous, the state of jealousy [variant: ussuyā ussuyanā ussuyitattaṃ (Ka.)] – this is called the fetter of envy. ^1-1126

Therein, what is the fetter of stinginess? The five kinds of stinginess – stinginess about dwellings, stinginess about families, stinginess about gain, stinginess about praise, stinginess about the Teaching. Whatever stinginess of this kind there is, being stingy, the state of being stingy, meanness, miserliness, tight-fistedness, the mind's tightness – this is called the fetter of stinginess. ^1-1127

Therein, what is the fetter of ignorance? Lack of knowledge of suffering, lack of knowledge of the arising of suffering, lack of knowledge of the cessation of suffering, lack of knowledge of the way leading to the cessation of suffering, lack of knowledge of the past, lack of knowledge of the future, lack of knowledge of both the past and the future, lack of knowledge of specific conditionality and dependently arisen states; whatever lack of knowledge of this kind there is, lack of vision, non-realisation, lack of understanding, lack of awakening, non-penetration, failure to take in, failure to fathom, failure to consider, failure to review, failure to see for oneself, stupidity, foolishness, lack of clear awareness, delusion, deep delusion, utter delusion, ignorance, the flood of ignorance, the yoke of ignorance, the underlying tendency to ignorance, the obsession of ignorance, the bar of ignorance, delusion, the unwholesome root – this is called the fetter of ignorance.
These states are fetters. ^1-1128

What are states that are not fetters? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not fetters. ^1-1129

What are states subject to fetters? Wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states subject to fetters. ^1-1130

What are states not subject to fetters? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states not subject to fetters. ^1-1131

What are states associated with fetters? The states that are associated with those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states associated with fetters. ^1-1132

What are states dissociated from fetters? The states that are dissociated from those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from fetters. ^1-1133

What are states that are both fetters and subject to fetters? Those same fetters are states that are both fetters and subject to fetters. ^1-1134

What are states that are subject to fetters but are not fetters? Among the states that, in relation to those states, are subject to fetters, except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are subject to fetters but are not fetters. ^1-1135

What are states that are both fetters and associated with fetters? The fetter of sensual lust is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of ignorance, the fetter of ignorance is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of sensual lust, the fetter of aversion is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of ignorance, the fetter of ignorance is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of aversion, the fetter of conceit is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of ignorance, the fetter of ignorance is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of conceit, the fetter of views is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of ignorance, the fetter of ignorance is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of views, the fetter of doubt is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of ignorance, the fetter of ignorance is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of doubt, the fetter of misapprehension of rules and observances is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of ignorance, the fetter of ignorance is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of misapprehension of rules and observances, the fetter of lust for existence is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of ignorance, the fetter of ignorance is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of lust for existence, the fetter of envy is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of ignorance, the fetter of ignorance is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of envy, the fetter of stinginess is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of ignorance, the fetter of ignorance is both a fetter and associated with a fetter through the fetter of stinginess – these are states that are both fetters and associated with fetters. ^1-1136

What are states that are associated with fetters but are not fetters? Among the states that are associated with those states, except those states, the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are associated with fetters but are not fetters. ^1-1137

What are states dissociated from fetters but subject to fetters? The states that are dissociated from those states and are wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere, the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states dissociated from fetters but subject to fetters. ^1-1138

What are states dissociated from fetters and not subject to fetters? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from fetters and not subject to fetters. ^1-1139

##### The Group on Ties ^1-3-2-5-0

What are states that are ties? The four ties – the bodily tie of covetousness, the bodily tie of ill will, the bodily tie of misapprehension of rules and observances, the bodily tie of dogmatic belief that this alone is true. ^1-1140

Therein, what is the bodily tie of covetousness? Whatever is lust, passion, compliance, accommodation, delight, delight and lust, passion of mind, wanting, infatuation, holding on, eagerness, intense eagerness, attachment, the mire, agitation, deceit, the begetter, the producer, the seamstress, the net-maker, the stream, the entangler, the thread, the spreading one, the accumulator, the companion, aspiration, the conduit to existence, the forest, the undergrowth, intimacy, affection, concern, kinship, hope, hoping, the state of hoping, hope for visible forms, hope for sounds, hope for smells, hope for tastes, hope for touches, hope for gain, hope for wealth, hope for children, hope for life, hankering, intense hankering, extreme hankering, hankering, the act of hankering, the state of hankering, voracity, being voracious, the state of voracity, fawning, the wish for fine things, unrighteous lust, lawless greed, liking, desiring, longing, envying, earnest longing, sensual craving, craving for continued existence, craving for non-existence, craving for fine-material existence, craving for immaterial existence, craving for cessation, craving for visible forms, craving for sounds, craving for smells, craving for tastes, craving for touches, craving for mental objects, the flood, the yoke, the tie, clinging, the obstruction, the hindrance, the covering, the bond, the corruption, the underlying tendency, the obsession, the creeper, meanness, the root of suffering, the source of suffering, the origin of suffering, Māra's snare, Māra's hook, Māra's domain, the river of craving, the net of craving, the leash of craving, the ocean of craving, covetousness, greed, the unwholesome root – this is called the bodily tie of covetousness. ^1-1141

Therein, what is the bodily tie of ill will? Annoyance arises at the thought "He has done me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He is doing me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He will do me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He has done harm to someone dear and pleasing to me… he is doing harm… he will do harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He has done good to someone not dear and not pleasing to me… he is doing good… he will do good", or annoyance arises without reason. Whatever annoyance of mind of this kind there is, resentment, aversion, antagonism, irritation, exasperation, fury, hate, hatred, intense hatred, malice of mind, mental hatred, anger, being angry, the state of being angry, hate, hating, the state of hating, malice, being malicious, the state of being malicious, hostility, antagonism, fierceness, abusiveness, discontent of mind – this is called the bodily tie of ill will. ^1-1142

Therein, what is the bodily tie of misapprehension of rules and observances? Ascetics and brahmins outside this Teaching hold that "Purification comes through rules, purification comes through observances, purification comes through rules and observances"; whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views, the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called the bodily tie of misapprehension of rules and observances. ^1-1143

Therein, what is the bodily tie of dogmatic belief that this alone is true? "The world is eternal; only this is true, anything else is false", or "The world is not eternal; only this is true, anything else is false", or "The world has an end; only this is true, anything else is false", or "The world has no end; only this is true, anything else is false", or "The soul and the body are the same; only this is true, anything else is false", or "The soul is one thing and the body another; only this is true, anything else is false", or "The Tathāgata exists after death; only this is true, anything else is false", or "The Tathāgata does not exist after death; only this is true, anything else is false", or "The Tathāgata both exists and does not exist after death; only this is true, anything else is false", or "The Tathāgata neither exists nor does not exist after death; only this is true, anything else is false"; whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views, the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called the bodily tie of dogmatic belief that this alone is true. Except the bodily tie of misapprehension of rules and observances, all wrong view is the bodily tie of dogmatic belief that this alone is true.
These states are ties. ^1-1144

What are states that are not ties? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not ties. ^1-1145

What are states subject to ties? Wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states subject to ties. ^1-1146

What are states not subject to ties? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states not subject to ties. ^1-1147

What are states associated with ties? The states that are associated with those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states associated with ties. ^1-1148

What are states dissociated from ties? The states that are dissociated from those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from ties. ^1-1149

What are states that are both ties and subject to ties? Those same ties are states that are both ties and subject to ties. ^1-1150

What are states that are subject to ties but are not ties? Among the states that, in relation to those states, are subject to ties, except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are subject to ties but are not ties. ^1-1151

What are states that are both ties and associated with ties? The bodily tie of misapprehension of rules and observances is both a tie and associated with a tie through the bodily tie of covetousness, the bodily tie of covetousness is both a tie and associated with a tie through the bodily tie of misapprehension of rules and observances, the bodily tie of dogmatic belief that this alone is true is both a tie and associated with a tie through the bodily tie of covetousness, the bodily tie of covetousness is both a tie and associated with a tie through the bodily tie of dogmatic belief that this alone is true – these are states that are both ties and associated with ties. ^1-1152

What are states that are associated with ties but are not ties? Among the states that are associated with those states, except those states, the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are associated with ties but are not ties. ^1-1153

What are states dissociated from ties but subject to ties? The states that are dissociated from those states and are wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere, the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states dissociated from ties but subject to ties. ^1-1154

What are states dissociated from ties and not subject to ties? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from ties and not subject to ties. ^1-1155

##### The Group on Floods ^1-3-2-6-0

What are states that are floods? The four floods… these are states dissociated from floods but subject to floods. ^1-1156

##### The Group on Yokes ^1-3-2-7-0

What are states that are yokes? The four yokes… these are states dissociated from yokes but subject to yokes. ^1-1157

##### The Group on Hindrances ^1-3-2-8-0

What are states that are hindrances? The six hindrances [variant: nīvaraṇāni (Syā.)] – the hindrance of sensual desire, the hindrance of ill will, the hindrance of sloth and torpor, the hindrance of restlessness and remorse, the hindrance of doubt, the hindrance of ignorance. ^1-1158

Therein, what is the hindrance of sensual desire? Whatever, with regard to sensual pleasures, is sensual desire, sensual lust, sensual delight, sensual craving, sensual affection, sensual fever, sensual infatuation, holding on to sensual pleasures – this is called the hindrance of sensual desire. ^1-1159

Therein, what is the hindrance of ill will? Annoyance arises at the thought "He has done me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He is doing me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He will do me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He has done harm to someone dear and pleasing to me… he is doing harm… he will do harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He has done good to someone not dear and not pleasing to me… he is doing good… he will do good", or annoyance arises without reason. Whatever annoyance of mind of this kind there is, resentment, aversion, antagonism, irritation, exasperation, fury, hate, hatred, intense hatred, malice of mind, mental hatred, anger, being angry, the state of being angry, hate, hating, the state of hating, malice, being malicious, the state of being malicious, hostility, antagonism, fierceness, abusiveness, discontent of mind – this is called the hindrance of ill will. ^1-1160

Therein, what is the hindrance of sloth and torpor? There is sloth, and there is torpor. ^1-1161

Therein, what is sloth? Whatever is indisposition of mind, unworkability, drooping, sagging, sluggishness, being sluggish, the state of being sluggish, sloth, being slothful, the slothful state of mind – this is called sloth. ^1-1162

Therein, what is torpor? Whatever is indisposition of the mental body, unworkability, enveloping, full enveloping, inner confinement, torpor, sleep, drowsiness, sleep, sleeping, the state of being asleep – this is called torpor. So this sloth and this torpor – this is called the hindrance of sloth and torpor. ^1-1163

Therein, what is the hindrance of restlessness and remorse? There is restlessness, and there is remorse. ^1-1164

Therein, what is restlessness? Whatever is restlessness of mind, disquiet, distraction of the mind, agitation of mind – this is called restlessness. ^1-1165

Therein, what is remorse? Perceiving what is not allowable as allowable, perceiving what is allowable as not allowable, perceiving what is blameless as blameworthy, perceiving what is blameworthy as blameless. Whatever remorse of this kind there is, being remorseful, the state of being remorseful, regret of mind, mental scratching – this is called remorse. So this restlessness and this remorse – this is called the hindrance of restlessness and remorse. ^1-1166

Therein, what is the hindrance of doubt? One is uncertain and in doubt about the Teacher, uncertain and in doubt about the Teaching, uncertain and in doubt about the Community, uncertain and in doubt about the training, uncertain and in doubt about the past, uncertain and in doubt about the future, uncertain and in doubt about both the past and the future, uncertain and in doubt about specific conditionality and dependently arisen states. Whatever uncertainty of this kind there is, being uncertain, the state of being uncertain, perplexity, doubt, indecision, a forked path, wavering, failure to take a definite stand, hesitation, faltering, failure to fathom, stiffness of mind, mental scratching – this is called the hindrance of doubt. ^1-1167

Therein, what is the hindrance of ignorance? Lack of knowledge about suffering, lack of knowledge of the arising of suffering, lack of knowledge of the cessation of suffering, lack of knowledge of the way leading to the cessation of suffering, lack of knowledge of the past, lack of knowledge of the future, lack of knowledge of both the past and the future, lack of knowledge of specific conditionality and dependently arisen states; whatever lack of knowledge of this kind there is, lack of vision, non-realisation, lack of understanding, lack of awakening, non-penetration, failure to take in, failure to fathom, failure to consider, failure to review, failure to see for oneself, stupidity, foolishness, lack of clear awareness, delusion, deep delusion, utter delusion, ignorance, the flood of ignorance, the yoke of ignorance, the underlying tendency to ignorance, the obsession of ignorance, the bar of ignorance, delusion, the unwholesome root – this is called the hindrance of ignorance.
These are states that are hindrances. ^1-1168

What are states that are not hindrances? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not hindrances. ^1-1169

What are states subject to hindrances? Wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states subject to hindrances. ^1-1170

What are states not subject to hindrances? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states not subject to hindrances. ^1-1171

What are states associated with hindrances? The states that are associated with those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states associated with hindrances. ^1-1172

What are states dissociated from hindrances? The states that are dissociated from those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from hindrances. ^1-1173

What are states that are both hindrances and subject to hindrances? Those same hindrances are states that are both hindrances and subject to hindrances. ^1-1174

What are states that are subject to hindrances but are not hindrances? Among the states that, in relation to those states, are subject to hindrances, except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are subject to hindrances but are not hindrances. ^1-1175

What are states that are both hindrances and associated with hindrances? The hindrance of sensual desire with the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance with the hindrance of sensual desire is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of ill will with the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance with the hindrance of ill will is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of sloth and torpor with the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance with the hindrance of sloth and torpor is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness with the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance with the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of remorse with the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance with the hindrance of remorse is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of doubt with the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance with the hindrance of doubt is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of sensual desire with the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness with the hindrance of sensual desire is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of ill will with the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness with the hindrance of ill will is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of sloth and torpor with the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness with the hindrance of sloth and torpor is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of remorse with the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness with the hindrance of remorse is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of doubt with the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness with the hindrance of doubt is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of ignorance with the hindrance of restlessness is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance; the hindrance of restlessness with the hindrance of ignorance is both a hindrance and associated with a hindrance – these are states that are both hindrances and associated with hindrances. ^1-1176

What are states that are associated with hindrances but are not hindrances? Among the states that are associated with those states, except those states, the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are associated with hindrances but are not hindrances. ^1-1177

What are states dissociated from hindrances but subject to hindrances? The states that are dissociated from those states and are wholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states dissociated from hindrances but subject to hindrances. ^1-1178

What are states dissociated from hindrances and not subject to hindrances? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from hindrances and not subject to hindrances. ^1-1179

##### The Group on Misapprehension ^1-3-2-9-0

What are states that are misapprehensions? Misapprehension through views. ^1-1180

Therein, what is misapprehension through views? "The world is eternal" or "The world is not eternal" or "The world has an end" or "The world has no end" or "The soul and the body are the same" or "The soul is one thing and the body another" or "The Tathāgata exists after death" or "The Tathāgata does not exist after death" or "The Tathāgata both exists and does not exist after death" or "The Tathāgata neither exists nor does not exist after death"; whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views, the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called misapprehension through views. All wrong view is misapprehension through views.
These are states that are misapprehensions. ^1-1181

What are states that are not misapprehensions? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not misapprehensions. ^1-1182

What are misapprehended states? Wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are misapprehended states. ^1-1183

What are states that are not misapprehended? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not misapprehended. ^1-1184

What are states associated with misapprehension? The states that are associated with those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states associated with misapprehension. ^1-1185

What are states dissociated from misapprehension? The states that are dissociated from those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from misapprehension. ^1-1186

What are states that are both misapprehensions and misapprehended? That same misapprehension is one of the states that are both misapprehensions and misapprehended. ^1-1187

What are states that are misapprehended but are not misapprehensions? Among the states that, in relation to those states, are misapprehended, except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are misapprehended but are not misapprehensions. ^1-1188

What are states dissociated from misapprehension but misapprehended? The states that are dissociated from those states and are wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints: those of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere and of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states dissociated from misapprehension but misapprehended. ^1-1189

What are states dissociated from misapprehension and not misapprehended? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from misapprehension and not misapprehended. ^1-1190

##### The Long Intermediate Pairs ^1-3-2-10-0

What are states that have an object? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that have an object. ^1-1191

What are states without an object? All material form and the unconditioned element – these are states without an object. ^1-1192

What are states that are mind? Eye-consciousness, ear-consciousness, nose-consciousness, tongue-consciousness, body-consciousness, the mind element, the mind-consciousness element – these are states that are mind. ^1-1193

What are states that are not mind? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not mind. ^1-1194

What are states that are mental factors? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations – these are states that are mental factors. ^1-1195
