What are states that are not mental factors? Mind, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not mental factors. ^1-1196

What are states associated with mind? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations – these are states associated with mind. ^1-1197

What are states dissociated from mind? All material form and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from mind. Mind should not be described – either as "associated with mind" or as "dissociated from mind". ^1-1198

What are states joined with mind? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations – these are states joined with mind. ^1-1199

What are states not joined with mind? All material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not joined with mind. Mind should not be described – either as "joined with mind" or as "not joined with mind". ^1-1200

What are states arising from mind? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations; bodily intimation, verbal intimation; or whatever other material form there is that is born of mind, caused by mind, arising from mind: the visible-form base, the sound base, the smell base, the taste base, the touch base, the space element, the water element, lightness of material form, pliancy of material form, workability of material form, growth of material form, continuity of material form, edible food – these are states arising from mind. ^1-1201

What are states not arising from mind? Mind, the remaining material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not arising from mind. ^1-1202

What are states existing together with mind? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, bodily intimation, verbal intimation – these are states existing together with mind. ^1-1203

What are states not existing together with mind? Mind, the remaining material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not existing together with mind. ^1-1204

What are states accompanying mind? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, bodily intimation, verbal intimation – these are states accompanying mind. ^1-1205

What are states not accompanying mind? Mind, the remaining material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not accompanying mind. ^1-1206

What are states joined with and arising from mind? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations – these are states joined with and arising from mind. ^1-1207

What are states not joined with and arising from mind? Mind, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not joined with and arising from mind. ^1-1208

What are states joined with, arising from and existing together with mind? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations – these are states joined with, arising from and existing together with mind. ^1-1209

What are states not joined with, arising from and existing together with mind? Mind, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not joined with, arising from and existing together with mind. ^1-1210

What are states joined with, arising from and accompanying mind? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations – these are states joined with, arising from and accompanying mind. ^1-1211

What are states not joined with, arising from and accompanying mind? Mind, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not joined with, arising from and accompanying mind. ^1-1212

What are internal states? The eye base… the mind base – these are internal states. ^1-1213

What are external states? The visible-form base… the mental-object base – these are external states. ^1-1214

What are derived states? The eye base… edible food – these are derived states. ^1-1215

What are states that are not derived? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, the aggregate of consciousness, the four great elements and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not derived. ^1-1216

What are states produced by past action? The results, subject to taints, of wholesome and unwholesome states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; and whatever material form is due to past action – these are states produced by past action. ^1-1217

What are states not produced by past action? Wholesome and unwholesome states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; the states that are functional, neither wholesome nor unwholesome nor results of action; whatever material form is not due to past action; the paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states not produced by past action. ^1-1218

##### The Group on Clinging ^1-3-2-11-0

What are states that are kinds of clinging? The four kinds of clinging – clinging to sensual pleasures, clinging to views, clinging to rules and observances, clinging to a doctrine of self [variant: kāmūpādānaṃ diṭṭhūpādānaṃ sīlabbatūpādānaṃ attavādūpādānaṃ (Sī.)]. ^1-1219

Therein, what is clinging to sensual pleasures? Whatever, with regard to sensual pleasures, is sensual desire, sensual lust, sensual delight, sensual craving, sensual affection, sensual fever, sensual infatuation, holding on to sensual pleasures – this is called clinging to sensual pleasures. ^1-1220

Therein, what is clinging to views? "There is nothing given, there is nothing offered, there is nothing sacrificed, there is no fruit or result of good and bad actions, this world does not exist, the other world does not exist, there is no mother, there is no father, there are no beings spontaneously reborn, there are in the world no ascetics and brahmins who have gone rightly [variant: samaggatā (Ka.)] and practise rightly and who, having realised this world and the other world by their own direct knowledge, make them known" – whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views, the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called clinging to views. Except clinging to rules and observances and clinging to a doctrine of self, all wrong view is clinging to views. ^1-1221

Therein, what is clinging to rules and observances? Ascetics and brahmins outside this Teaching hold that "Purification comes through rules, purification comes through observances, purification comes through rules and observances" – whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views, the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called clinging to rules and observances. ^1-1222

Therein, what is clinging to a doctrine of self? Here an uninstructed ordinary person, who does not see the noble ones, is unskilled in the teaching of the noble ones and undisciplined in the teaching of the noble ones, who does not see good people, is unskilled in the teaching of good people and undisciplined in the teaching of good people, regards material form as self, or self as possessing material form, or material form as in self, or self as in material form. He regards feeling… perception… mental formations… consciousness as self, or self as possessing consciousness, or consciousness as in self, or self as in consciousness. Whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views, the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called clinging to a doctrine of self.
These are states that are kinds of clinging. ^1-1223

What are states that are not kinds of clinging? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not kinds of clinging. ^1-1224

What are states subject to clinging? Wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states subject to clinging. ^1-1225

What are states not subject to clinging? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states not subject to clinging. ^1-1226

What are states associated with clinging? The states that are associated with those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states associated with clinging. ^1-1227

What are states dissociated from clinging? The states that are dissociated from those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from clinging. ^1-1228

What are states that are both kinds of clinging and subject to clinging? Those same kinds of clinging are states that are both kinds of clinging and subject to clinging. ^1-1229

What are states that are subject to clinging but are not kinds of clinging? Among the states that, in relation to those states, are subject to clinging, except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are subject to clinging but are not kinds of clinging. ^1-1230

What are states that are both kinds of clinging and associated with clinging? Clinging to views with clinging to sensual pleasures is both a kind of clinging and associated with clinging; clinging to sensual pleasures with clinging to views is both a kind of clinging and associated with clinging; clinging to rules and observances with clinging to sensual pleasures is both a kind of clinging and associated with clinging; clinging to sensual pleasures with clinging to rules and observances is both a kind of clinging and associated with clinging; clinging to a doctrine of self with clinging to sensual pleasures is both a kind of clinging and associated with clinging; clinging to sensual pleasures with clinging to a doctrine of self is both a kind of clinging and associated with clinging – these are states that are both kinds of clinging and associated with clinging. ^1-1231

What are states that are associated with clinging but are not kinds of clinging? Among the states that are associated with those states, except those states, the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are associated with clinging but are not kinds of clinging. ^1-1232

What are states dissociated from clinging but subject to clinging? The states that are dissociated from those states and are wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states dissociated from clinging but subject to clinging. ^1-1233

What are states dissociated from clinging and not subject to clinging? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from clinging and not subject to clinging.
The second recitation section of the chapter of summaries. ^1-1234

##### The Group on Defilements ^1-3-2-12-0

What are states that are defilements? The ten bases of defilement – greed, hate, delusion, conceit, view, doubt, sloth, restlessness, shamelessness, fearlessness of wrongdoing. ^1-1235

Therein, what is greed? Whatever is lust, passion, compliance, accommodation, delight, delight and lust, passion of mind, wanting, infatuation, holding on, eagerness, intense eagerness, attachment, the mire, agitation, deceit, the begetter, the producer, the seamstress, the net-maker, the stream, the entangler, the thread, the spreading one, the accumulator, the companion, aspiration, the conduit to existence, the forest, the undergrowth, intimacy, affection, concern, kinship, hope, hoping, the state of hoping, hope for visible forms, hope for sounds, hope for smells, hope for tastes, hope for touches, hope for gain, hope for wealth, hope for children, hope for life, hankering, intense hankering, extreme hankering, hankering, the act of hankering, the state of hankering, voracity, being voracious, the state of voracity, fawning, the wish for fine things, unrighteous lust, lawless greed, liking, desiring, longing, envying, earnest longing, sensual craving, craving for continued existence, craving for non-existence, craving for fine-material existence, craving for immaterial existence, craving for cessation, craving for visible forms, craving for sounds, craving for smells, craving for tastes, craving for touches, craving for mental objects, the flood, the yoke, the tie, clinging, the obstruction, the hindrance, the covering, the bond, the corruption, the underlying tendency, the obsession, the creeper, meanness, the root of suffering, the source of suffering, the origin of suffering, Māra's snare, Māra's hook, Māra's domain, the river of craving, the net of craving, the leash of craving, the ocean of craving, covetousness, greed, the unwholesome root – this is called greed. ^1-1236

Therein, what is hate? Annoyance arises at the thought "He has done me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He is doing me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He will do me harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He has done harm to someone dear and pleasing to me… he is doing harm… he will do harm", annoyance arises at the thought "He has done good to someone not dear and not pleasing to me… he is doing good… he will do good", or annoyance arises without reason. Whatever annoyance of mind of this kind there is, resentment, aversion, antagonism, irritation, exasperation, fury, hate, hatred, intense hatred, malice of mind, mental hatred, anger, being angry, the state of being angry, hate, hating, the state of hating, malice, being malicious, the state of being malicious, hostility, antagonism, fierceness, abusiveness, discontent of mind – this is called hate. ^1-1237

Therein, what is delusion? Lack of knowledge about suffering, lack of knowledge of the arising of suffering, lack of knowledge of the cessation of suffering, lack of knowledge of the way leading to the cessation of suffering, lack of knowledge of the past, lack of knowledge of the future, lack of knowledge of both the past and the future, lack of knowledge of specific conditionality and dependently arisen states. Whatever lack of knowledge of this kind there is, lack of vision, non-realisation, lack of understanding, lack of awakening, non-penetration, failure to take in, failure to fathom, failure to consider, failure to review, failure to see for oneself, stupidity, foolishness, lack of clear awareness, delusion, deep delusion, utter delusion, ignorance, the flood of ignorance, the yoke of ignorance, the underlying tendency to ignorance, the obsession of ignorance, the bar of ignorance, delusion, the unwholesome root – this is called delusion. ^1-1238

Therein, what is conceit? Conceit that "I am better", conceit that "I am equal", conceit that "I am inferior"; whatever conceit of this kind there is, conceiving, the state of having conceived, loftiness, haughtiness, flag-raising, self-exaltation, the mind's wish to fly its banner – this is called conceit. ^1-1239

Therein, what is view? "The world is eternal" or "The world is not eternal" or "The world has an end" or "The world has no end" or "The soul and the body are the same" or "The soul is one thing and the body another" or "The Tathāgata exists after death" or "The Tathāgata does not exist after death" or "The Tathāgata both exists and does not exist after death" or "The Tathāgata neither exists nor does not exist after death"; whatever view of this kind there is, holding views, the thicket of views, the wilderness of views, the contortion of views, the vacillation of views, the fetter of views, grasping, fixation, adherence, misapprehension, a wrong road, a wrong path, wrongness, a basis for sectarian doctrines, a distorted grasp – this is called view. All wrong view is view. ^1-1240

Therein, what is doubt? One is uncertain and in doubt about the Teacher, uncertain and in doubt about the Teaching, uncertain and in doubt about the Community, uncertain and in doubt about the training, uncertain and in doubt about the past, uncertain and in doubt about the future, uncertain and in doubt about both the past and the future, uncertain and in doubt about specific conditionality and dependently arisen states; whatever uncertainty of this kind there is, being uncertain, the state of being uncertain, perplexity, doubt, indecision, a forked path, wavering, failure to take a definite stand, hesitation, faltering, failure to fathom, stiffness of mind, mental scratching – this is called doubt. ^1-1241

Therein, what is sloth? Whatever is indisposition of mind, unworkability, drooping, sagging, sluggishness, being sluggish, the state of being sluggish, sloth, being slothful, the slothful state of mind – this is called sloth. ^1-1242

Therein, what is restlessness? Whatever is restlessness of mind, disquiet, distraction of the mind, agitation of mind – this is called restlessness. ^1-1243

Therein, what is shamelessness? Not being ashamed of what one should be ashamed of, not being ashamed of engaging in bad, unwholesome states – this is called shamelessness. ^1-1244

Therein, what is fearlessness of wrongdoing? Not fearing what one should fear, not fearing engaging in bad, unwholesome states – this is called fearlessness of wrongdoing.
These are states that are defilements. ^1-1245

What are states that are not defilements? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not defilements. ^1-1246

What are states subject to defilement? Wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states subject to defilement. ^1-1247

What are states not subject to defilement? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states not subject to defilement. ^1-1248

What are defiled states? The three unwholesome roots – greed, hate, delusion; the defilements that occur together with them, the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness, and the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are defiled states. ^1-1249

What are undefiled states? The wholesome and indeterminate states: those of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere, and those not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are undefiled states. ^1-1250

What are states associated with defilements? The states that are associated with those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states associated with defilements. ^1-1251

What are states dissociated from defilements? The states that are dissociated from those states: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from defilements. ^1-1252

What are states that are both defilements and subject to defilement? Those same defilements are states that are both defilements and subject to defilement. ^1-1253

What are states that are subject to defilement but are not defilements? Among the states that, in relation to those states, are subject to defilement, except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are subject to defilement but are not defilements. ^1-1254

What are states that are both defilements and defiled? Those same defilements are states that are both defilements and defiled. ^1-1255

What are states that are defiled but are not defilements? Among the states that, in relation to those states, are defiled, except those states, the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are defiled but are not defilements. ^1-1256

What are states that are both defilements and associated with defilements? Greed with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with greed is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; hate with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with hate is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; conceit with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with conceit is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; view with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with view is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; doubt with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with doubt is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; sloth with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with sloth is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; greed with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with greed is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; hate with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with hate is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; conceit with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with conceit is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; view with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with view is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; doubt with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with doubt is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; sloth with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with sloth is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; greed with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with greed is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; hate with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with hate is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; conceit with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with conceit is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; view with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with view is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; doubt with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with doubt is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; sloth with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with sloth is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; greed with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with greed is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; hate with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with hate is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; delusion with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with delusion is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; conceit with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with conceit is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; view with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with view is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; doubt with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with doubt is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; sloth with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with sloth is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; restlessness with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with restlessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; shamelessness with fearlessness of wrongdoing is both a defilement and associated with a defilement; fearlessness of wrongdoing with shamelessness is both a defilement and associated with a defilement – these are states that are both defilements and associated with defilements. ^1-1257

What are states that are associated with defilements but are not defilements? Among the states that are associated with those states, except those states, the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states that are associated with defilements but are not defilements. ^1-1258

What are states dissociated from defilements but subject to defilement? The states that are dissociated from those states and are wholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states dissociated from defilements but subject to defilement. ^1-1259

What are states dissociated from defilements and not subject to defilement? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states dissociated from defilements and not subject to defilement. ^1-1260

##### The Closing Pairs ^1-3-2-13-0

What are states to be abandoned by seeing? The three fetters – identity view, doubt, misapprehension of rules and observances. ^1-1261

Therein, what is identity view? Here an uninstructed ordinary person, who does not see the noble ones, is unskilled in the teaching of the noble ones and undisciplined in the teaching of the noble ones, who does not see good people, is unskilled in the teaching of good people and undisciplined in the teaching of good people, regards material form as self, or self as possessing material form, or material form as in self, or self as in material form. He regards feeling… perception… mental formations… consciousness as self, or self as possessing consciousness, or consciousness as in self, or self as in consciousness. Whatever view of this kind there is, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called identity view. ^1-1262

Therein, what is doubt? One is uncertain and in doubt about the Teacher… stiffness of mind, mental scratching – this is called doubt. ^1-1263

Therein, what is misapprehension of rules and observances? Ascetics and brahmins outside this Teaching hold that "Purification comes through rules, purification comes through observances, purification comes through rules and observances" – whatever view of this kind there is, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called misapprehension of rules and observances. These three fetters, the defilements that occur together with them, the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness, and the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1264

What are states not to be abandoned by seeing? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1265

What are states to be abandoned by development? The remaining greed, hate, delusion, the defilements that occur together with them, the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness, and the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states to be abandoned by development. ^1-1266

What are states not to be abandoned by development? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not to be abandoned by development. ^1-1267

What are states whose roots are to be abandoned by seeing? The three fetters – identity view, doubt, misapprehension of rules and observances. ^1-1268

Therein, what is identity view? Here an uninstructed ordinary person, who does not see the noble ones, is unskilled in the teaching of the noble ones and undisciplined in the teaching of the noble ones, who does not see good people, is unskilled in the teaching of good people and undisciplined in the teaching of good people, regards material form as self, or self as possessing material form, or material form as in self, or self as in material form. He regards feeling… perception… mental formations… consciousness as self, or self as possessing consciousness, or consciousness as in self, or self as in consciousness. Whatever view of this kind there is, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called identity view. ^1-1269

Therein, what is doubt? One is uncertain and in doubt about the Teacher… stiffness of mind, mental scratching – this is called doubt. ^1-1270

Therein, what is misapprehension of rules and observances? Ascetics and brahmins outside this Teaching hold that "Purification comes through rules, purification comes through observances, purification comes through rules and observances" – whatever view of this kind there is, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called misapprehension of rules and observances. These three fetters, the defilements that occur together with them, the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness, and the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states whose roots are to be abandoned by seeing. The three fetters – identity view, doubt, misapprehension of rules and observances – these are states to be abandoned by seeing. The greed, hate and delusion that occur together with them – these are roots to be abandoned by seeing. The defilements that occur together with them, the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness, and the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states whose roots are to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1271

What are states whose roots are not to be abandoned by seeing? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states whose roots are not to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1272

What are states whose roots are to be abandoned by development? The remaining greed, hate, delusion – these are roots to be abandoned by development. The defilements that occur together with them, the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness, and the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states whose roots are to be abandoned by development. ^1-1273

What are states whose roots are not to be abandoned by development? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states whose roots are not to be abandoned by development. ^1-1274

What are states with applied thought? In the plane with applied thought, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or not included in the spheres of existence, except applied thought, the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness – these are states with applied thought. ^1-1275

What are states without applied thought? In the plane without applied thought, in the sense sphere, the fine-material sphere, the immaterial sphere and what is not included in the spheres of existence: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; applied thought, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states without applied thought. ^1-1276

What are states with sustained thought? In the plane with sustained thought, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or not included in the spheres of existence, except sustained thought, the associated aggregate of feeling, aggregate of perception, aggregate of mental formations, aggregate of consciousness – these are states with sustained thought. ^1-1277

What are states without sustained thought? In the plane without sustained thought, in the sense sphere, the fine-material sphere, the immaterial sphere and what is not included in the spheres of existence: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; sustained thought, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states without sustained thought. ^1-1278

What are states with rapture? In the plane with rapture, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or not included in the spheres of existence, except rapture, the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness – these are states with rapture. ^1-1279

What are states without rapture? In the plane without rapture, in the sense sphere, the fine-material sphere, the immaterial sphere and what is not included in the spheres of existence: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; rapture, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states without rapture. ^1-1280

What are states accompanied by rapture? In the plane of rapture, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or not included in the spheres of existence, except rapture, the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness – these are states accompanied by rapture. ^1-1281

What are states not accompanied by rapture? In what is not the plane of rapture, in the sense sphere, the fine-material sphere, the immaterial sphere and what is not included in the spheres of existence: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; rapture, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not accompanied by rapture. ^1-1282

What are states accompanied by happiness? In the plane of happiness, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or not included in the spheres of existence, except happiness, the associated aggregate of perception, aggregate of mental formations, aggregate of consciousness – these are states accompanied by happiness. ^1-1283

What are states not accompanied by happiness? In what is not the plane of happiness, in the sense sphere, the fine-material sphere, the immaterial sphere and what is not included in the spheres of existence: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; happiness, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not accompanied by happiness. ^1-1284

What are states accompanied by equanimity? In the plane of equanimity, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence, except equanimity, the associated aggregate of perception, aggregate of mental formations, aggregate of consciousness – these are states accompanied by equanimity. ^1-1285

What are states not accompanied by equanimity? In what is not the plane of equanimity, in the sense sphere, the fine-material sphere and what is not included in the spheres of existence: the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; equanimity, all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states not accompanied by equanimity. ^1-1286

What are states of the sense sphere? Taking the Avīci hell as the boundary below, and including above the gods who wield power over the creations of others [variant: paranimmitavasavattideve (Sī., Ka.)], whatever aggregates, elements and sense bases move within this range and are included in it: material form, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness – these are states of the sense sphere. ^1-1287

What are states not of the sense sphere? States of the fine-material sphere, states of the immaterial sphere, states not included in the spheres of existence – these are states not of the sense sphere. ^1-1288

What are states of the fine-material sphere? Taking the Brahma world as the boundary below, and including above the Akaniṭṭha gods [variant: akaniṭṭhadeve (Sī., Ka.)], whatever states of mind and mental factors move within this range and are included in it, of one who has entered meditative attainment, or of one who has been reborn there, or of one who dwells happily in this very life [variant: diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārassa (Ka.)] – these are states of the fine-material sphere. ^1-1289

What are states not of the fine-material sphere? States of the sense sphere, states of the immaterial sphere, states not included in the spheres of existence – these are states not of the fine-material sphere. ^1-1290

What are states of the immaterial sphere? Taking as the boundary below the gods who have reached the base of infinite space, and including above the gods who have reached the base of neither perception nor non-perception, whatever states of mind and mental factors move within this range and are included in it, of one who has entered meditative attainment, or of one who has been reborn there, or of one who dwells happily in this very life – these are states of the immaterial sphere. ^1-1291

What are states not of the immaterial sphere? States of the sense sphere, states of the fine-material sphere, states not included in the spheres of existence – these are states not of the immaterial sphere. ^1-1292

What are states included in the spheres of existence? Wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are states included in the spheres of existence. ^1-1293

What are states not included in the spheres of existence? The paths, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are states not included in the spheres of existence. ^1-1294

What are states that lead to liberation? The four paths not included in the spheres of existence – these are states that lead to liberation. ^1-1295

What are states that do not lead to liberation? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that do not lead to liberation. ^1-1296

What are fixed states? The five actions with immediate result, the wrong view that is fixed, and the four paths not included in the spheres of existence – these are fixed states. ^1-1297

What are states that are not fixed? Except those states, the remaining wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere or not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states that are not fixed. ^1-1298

What are surpassable states? Wholesome, unwholesome and indeterminate states subject to taints, whether of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere or of the immaterial sphere; the aggregate of material form… the aggregate of consciousness – these are surpassable states. ^1-1299

What are unsurpassable states? The paths not included in the spheres of existence, the fruits of the paths, and the unconditioned element – these are unsurpassable states. ^1-1300

What are states with conflict? The three unwholesome roots: greed, hate, delusion; the defilements that occur together with them, the associated aggregate of feeling… aggregate of consciousness, and the bodily action, verbal action and mental action arising from them – these are states with conflict. ^1-1301

What are states without conflict? The wholesome and indeterminate states: those of the sense sphere, of the fine-material sphere, of the immaterial sphere, and those not included in the spheres of existence; the aggregate of feeling… the aggregate of consciousness; all material form and the unconditioned element – these are states without conflict.
The Abhidhamma pairs. ^1-1302

#### Summary of the Discourse Pairs ^1-3-3-0

What are states that share in true knowledge? The states associated with true knowledge – these are states that share in true knowledge. ^1-1303

What are states that share in ignorance? The states associated with ignorance – these are states that share in ignorance. ^1-1304

What are states like lightning? Wisdom in the three lower noble paths – these are states like lightning. ^1-1305

What are states like a diamond? Wisdom in the highest path, the path of arahantship – these are states like a diamond. ^1-1306

What are foolish states? Shamelessness and fearlessness of wrongdoing – these are foolish states. All unwholesome states are foolish. ^1-1307

What are wise states? Moral shame and fear of wrongdoing – these are wise states. All wholesome states are wise. ^1-1308

What are dark states? Shamelessness and fearlessness of wrongdoing – these are dark states. All unwholesome states are dark. ^1-1309

What are bright states? Moral shame and fear of wrongdoing – these are bright states? All wholesome states are bright. ^1-1310

What are states that cause torment? Bodily misconduct, verbal misconduct, mental misconduct – these are states that cause torment. All unwholesome states cause torment. ^1-1311

What are states that do not cause torment? Good bodily conduct, good verbal conduct, good mental conduct – these are states that do not cause torment. All wholesome states are states that do not cause torment. ^1-1312

What are states that are names? Whatever reckoning, label, designation, usage, name, naming, appellation, expression, term or utterance there is for each of those states – these are states that are names. All states are pathways for names. ^1-1313

What are states that are expressions? Whatever reckoning, label, designation, usage, name, naming, appellation, expression, term or utterance there is for each of those states – these are states that are expressions. All states are pathways for expressions. ^1-1314

What are states that are designations? Whatever reckoning, label, designation, usage, name, naming, appellation, expression, term or utterance there is for each of those states – these are states that are designations. All states are pathways for designations. ^1-1315

Therein, what is mentality? The aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, the aggregate of mental formations, the aggregate of consciousness, and the unconditioned element – this is called mentality. ^1-1316

Therein, what is material form? The four great elements and the material form derived from the four great elements – this is called material form. ^1-1317

Therein, what is ignorance? Whatever lack of knowledge there is, lack of vision… the bar of ignorance, delusion, the unwholesome root – this is called ignorance. ^1-1318

Therein, what is craving for continued existence? Whatever, with regard to states of existence, is desire for existence… holding on to existence – this is called craving for continued existence. ^1-1319

Therein, what is the view of continued existence? "The self and the world will exist", whatever view of this kind there is, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called the view of continued existence. ^1-1320

Therein, what is the view of non-existence? "The self and the world will not exist", whatever view of this kind there is, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called the view of non-existence. ^1-1321

Therein, what is the eternalist view? "The self and the world are eternal", whatever view of this kind there is, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called the eternalist view. ^1-1322

Therein, what is the annihilationist view? "The self and the world will be annihilated", whatever view of this kind there is, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called the annihilationist view. ^1-1323

Therein, what is the view that there is an end? "The self and the world have an end", whatever view of this kind there is, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called the view that there is an end. ^1-1324

Therein, what is the view that there is no end? "The self and the world have no end", whatever view of this kind there is, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called the view that there is no end. ^1-1325

Therein, what is the view concerning the past? Whatever view arises concerning the past, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called the view concerning the past. ^1-1326

Therein, what is the view concerning the future? Whatever view arises concerning the future, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called the view concerning the future. ^1-1327

Therein, what is shamelessness? Not being ashamed of what one should be ashamed of, not being ashamed of engaging in bad, unwholesome states – this is called shamelessness. ^1-1328

Therein, what is fearlessness of wrongdoing? Not fearing what one should fear, not fearing engaging in bad, unwholesome states – this is called fearlessness of wrongdoing. ^1-1329

Therein, what is moral shame? Being ashamed of what one should be ashamed of, being ashamed of engaging in bad, unwholesome states – this is called moral shame. ^1-1330

Therein, what is fear of wrongdoing? Fearing what one should fear, fearing engaging in bad, unwholesome states – this is called fear of wrongdoing. ^1-1331

Therein, what is being hard to correct? When one is spoken to in accordance with the Teaching, intractability, unruliness, being hard to correct, taking things the opposite way, delight in opposing, disregard, disregarding, disrespect, lack of deference – this is called being hard to correct. ^1-1332

Therein, what is having bad friends? Those persons who are without faith, immoral, of little learning, stingy and unwise: whatever associating with them there is, frequenting them, keeping company with them, resorting to them, closely resorting to them, devotion to them, close devotion to them, leaning towards them – this is called having bad friends. ^1-1333

Therein, what is being easy to correct? When one is spoken to in accordance with the Teaching, tractability, compliance, being easy to correct, not taking things the opposite way, not delighting in opposing, respect, regard, regarding, deference – this is called being easy to correct. ^1-1334

Therein, what is having good friends? Those persons who have faith, are moral, of much learning, generous and wise: whatever associating with them there is, frequenting them, keeping company with them, resorting to them, closely resorting to them, devotion to them, close devotion to them, leaning towards them – this is called having good friends. ^1-1335

Therein, what is skill regarding offences? The five classes of offences are offences, and the seven classes of offences are offences. Whatever skill regarding offences there is with respect to those offences, wisdom, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called skill regarding offences. ^1-1336

Therein, what is skill in emerging from offences? Whatever skill in emerging from those offences there is, wisdom, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called skill in emerging from offences. ^1-1337

Therein, what is skill in meditative attainments? There is meditative attainment with applied thought and sustained thought, there is meditative attainment without applied thought but with sustained thought only, there is meditative attainment without applied thought and without sustained thought. Whatever skill in meditative attainments there is with respect to those attainments, wisdom, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called skill in meditative attainments. ^1-1338

Therein, what is skill in emerging from meditative attainments? Whatever skill in emerging from those attainments there is, wisdom, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called skill in emerging from meditative attainments. ^1-1339

Therein, what is skill in the elements? The eighteen elements: the eye element, the visible-form element, the eye-consciousness element, the ear element, the sound element, the ear-consciousness element, the nose element, the smell element, the nose-consciousness element, the tongue element, the taste element, the tongue-consciousness element, the body element, the touch element, the body-consciousness element, the mind element, the element of mental objects, the mind-consciousness element. Whatever skill in the elements there is with respect to those elements, wisdom, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called skill in the elements. ^1-1340

Therein, what is skill in attention? Whatever skill in attention there is with respect to those elements, wisdom, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called skill in attention. ^1-1341

Therein, what is skill in the sense bases? The twelve sense bases – the eye base, the visible-form base, the ear base, the sound base, the nose base, the smell base, the tongue base, the taste base, the body base, the touch base, the mind base, the mental-object base. Whatever skill in the sense bases there is with respect to those sense bases, wisdom, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called skill in the sense bases. ^1-1342

Therein, what is skill in dependent origination? With ignorance as condition there are mental formations, with mental formations as condition there is consciousness, with consciousness as condition there are mentality and material form, with mentality and material form as condition there are the six sense bases, with the six sense bases as condition there is contact, with contact as condition there is feeling, with feeling as condition there is craving, with craving as condition there is clinging, with clinging as condition there is existence, with existence as condition there is birth, with birth as condition ageing and death, sorrow, lamentation, pain, sadness and despair come to be; thus there is the arising of this whole mass of suffering. Whatever wisdom there is in this, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called skill in dependent origination. ^1-1343

Therein, what is skill in what is possible? Whatever states are roots and conditions of whatever states for their arising [variant: upādāya (Sī., Ka.)], that in each case is what is possible; whatever wisdom there is in this, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called skill in what is possible. ^1-1344

Therein, what is skill in what is impossible? Whatever states are not roots and not conditions of whatever states for their arising, that in each case is what is impossible; whatever wisdom there is in this, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called skill in what is impossible. ^1-1345

Therein, what is straightforwardness? Whatever is straightforwardness, freedom from deviousness, freedom from crookedness, freedom from twistedness – this is called straightforwardness. ^1-1346

Therein, what is mildness? Whatever is pliancy, softness, freedom from hardness, freedom from rigidity, humility of mind – this is called mildness. ^1-1347

Therein, what is patience? Whatever is patience, forbearance, endurance, non-ferocity, non-abusiveness, contentment of mind – this is called patience. ^1-1348

Therein, what is gentleness? Whatever is non-transgression by body, non-transgression by speech, non-transgression by body and speech – this is called gentleness. All restraint by morality is gentleness. ^1-1349

Therein, what is courtesy? Having given up the kind of speech that is rough, harsh, hurtful to others, offensive to others, bordering on anger and not conducive to concentration, one speaks the kind of speech that is blameless, pleasing to the ear, lovable, going to the heart, polite, liked by many and pleasing to many; whatever smooth speech there is in this, courteous speech, non-harsh speech – this is called courtesy. ^1-1350

Therein, what is hospitality? There are two kinds of hospitality – hospitality with material things and hospitality with the Teaching. Here someone is hospitable, through hospitality with material things or through hospitality with the Teaching – this is called hospitality. ^1-1351

Therein, what is not guarding the doors of the senses? Here someone, on seeing a visible form with the eye, grasps at its general features and grasps at its details. Since, if he leaves the faculty of the eye unrestrained, bad, unwholesome states of covetousness and sadness might flow in upon him, he does not practise its restraint, he does not guard the faculty of the eye, he does not undertake the restraint of the faculty of the eye. On hearing a sound with the ear… on smelling a smell with the nose… on tasting a taste with the tongue… on touching a touch with the body… on cognising a mental object with the mind, he grasps at its general features and grasps at its details. Since, if he leaves the faculty of mind unrestrained, bad, unwholesome states of covetousness and sadness might flow in upon him, he does not practise its restraint, he does not guard the faculty of mind, he does not undertake the restraint of the faculty of mind. Whatever non-guarding, non-protection, non-watching, non-restraint there is of these six faculties – this is called not guarding the doors of the senses. ^1-1352

Therein, what is not knowing moderation in eating? Here someone, without reflecting and unwisely, takes food for amusement, for intoxication, for adornment, for beautification. Whatever there is in this of not being content, not knowing moderation and lack of reflection in eating – this is called not knowing moderation in eating. ^1-1353

Therein, what is guarding the doors of the senses? Here someone, on seeing a visible form with the eye, does not grasp at its general features and does not grasp at its details. Since, if he leaves the faculty of the eye unrestrained, bad, unwholesome states of covetousness and sadness might flow in upon him, he practises its restraint, he guards the faculty of the eye, he undertakes the restraint of the faculty of the eye. On hearing a sound with the ear… on smelling a smell with the nose… on tasting a taste with the tongue… on touching a touch with the body… on cognising a mental object with the mind, he does not grasp at its general features and does not grasp at its details. Since, if he leaves the faculty of mind unrestrained, bad, unwholesome states of covetousness and sadness might flow in upon him, he practises its restraint, he guards the faculty of mind, he undertakes the restraint of the faculty of mind. Whatever guarding, protection, watching, restraint there is of these six faculties – this is called guarding the doors of the senses. ^1-1354

Therein, what is knowing moderation in eating? Here someone, reflecting wisely, takes food – not for amusement, not for intoxication, not for adornment, not for beautification, but only for the support and maintenance of this body, for ending discomfort, for assisting the holy life, thinking, "Thus I shall put an end to old feeling and not give rise to new feeling, and I shall have continued life, blamelessness and living at ease." Whatever there is in this of being content, knowing moderation and reflection in eating – this is called knowing moderation in eating. ^1-1355

Therein, what is forgetfulness? Whatever is lack of mindfulness, lack of recollection, lack of recall, lack of mindfulness, not remembering, not keeping in mind, floating away, forgetting – this is called forgetfulness. ^1-1356

Therein, what is lack of clear awareness? Whatever lack of knowledge there is, lack of vision… the bar of ignorance, delusion, the unwholesome root – this is called lack of clear awareness. ^1-1357

Therein, what is mindfulness? Whatever is mindfulness, recollection, recall, mindfulness, remembering, keeping in mind, not floating away, not forgetting, mindfulness, the faculty of mindfulness, the power of mindfulness, right mindfulness – this is called mindfulness. ^1-1358

Therein, what is clear awareness? Whatever is wisdom, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called clear awareness. ^1-1359

Therein, what is the power of reflection? Whatever is wisdom, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called the power of reflection. ^1-1360

Therein, what is the power of development? Whatever is practice, development and repeated practice of wholesome states – this is called the power of development. The seven factors of awakening too are the power of development. ^1-1361

Therein, what is calm? Whatever is the stability of mind… right concentration – this is called calm. ^1-1362

Therein, what is insight? Whatever is wisdom, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called insight. ^1-1363

Therein, what is the sign of calm? Whatever is the stability of mind… right concentration – this is called the sign of calm. ^1-1364

Therein, what is the sign of exertion? Whatever is the mental arousing of energy… right effort – this is called the sign of exertion. ^1-1365

Therein, what is exertion? Whatever is the mental arousing of energy… right effort – this is called exertion. ^1-1366

Therein, what is non-distraction? Whatever is the stability of mind… right concentration – this is called non-distraction. ^1-1367

Therein, what is failure in morality? Whatever is transgression by body, transgression by speech, transgression by body and speech – this is called failure in morality. All immorality is failure in morality. ^1-1368

Therein, what is failure in view? "There is nothing given, there is nothing offered, there is nothing sacrificed, there is no fruit or result of good and bad actions, this world does not exist, the other world does not exist, there is no mother, there is no father, there are no beings spontaneously reborn, there are in the world no ascetics and brahmins who have gone rightly and practise rightly and who, having realised this world and the other world by their own direct knowledge, make them known"; whatever view of this kind there is, holding views… a distorted grasp – this is called failure in view. All wrong view is failure in view. ^1-1369

Therein, what is accomplishment in morality? Whatever is non-transgression by body, non-transgression by speech, non-transgression by body and speech – this is called accomplishment in morality. All restraint by morality is accomplishment in morality. ^1-1370

Therein, what is accomplishment in view? "There is what is given, there is what is offered, there is what is sacrificed, there is fruit and result of good and bad actions, there is this world, there is the other world, there is mother, there is father, there are beings spontaneously reborn, there are in the world ascetics and brahmins who have gone rightly and practise rightly and who, having realised this world and the other world by their own direct knowledge, make them known"; whatever wisdom of this kind there is, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view – this is called accomplishment in view. All right view is accomplishment in view. ^1-1371

Therein, what is purification of morality? Whatever is non-transgression by body, non-transgression by speech, non-transgression by body and speech – this is called purification of morality. All restraint by morality is purification of morality. ^1-1372

Therein, what is purification of view? Knowledge of the ownership of action, knowledge in conformity with the truths, the knowledge of one who possesses the path, the knowledge of one who possesses the fruit. ^1-1373

"Purification of view" – whatever is wisdom, understanding… non-delusion, investigation of states, right view. ^1-1374

"And effort in keeping with one's view" – whatever is the mental arousing of energy… right effort. ^1-1375

"A sense of urgency" – fear of birth, fear of ageing, fear of sickness, fear of death. "The things that should stir it" [variant: saṃvejanīyaṃ (Sī.)] – birth, ageing, sickness, death. ^1-1376

"And the wise effort of one so stirred" – here a bhikkhu, for the non-arising of bad, unwholesome states that have not yet arisen, generates desire, makes an effort, arouses energy, exerts the mind and strives; for the abandoning of bad, unwholesome states that have arisen, he generates desire, makes an effort, arouses energy, exerts the mind and strives; for the arising of wholesome states that have not yet arisen, he generates desire, makes an effort, arouses energy, exerts the mind and strives; for the continuance, non-decline, increase, abundance, development and fulfilment of wholesome states that have arisen, he generates desire, makes an effort, arouses energy, exerts the mind and strives. ^1-1377

"Not being content with wholesome states" – whatever wish for more there is in one who is not content in the development of wholesome states. ^1-1378

"Not holding back in effort" – whatever there is, in the development of wholesome states, of acting carefully, acting persistently, acting without stopping, not being sluggish, not putting down desire, not putting down the burden, practice, development and repeated practice. ^1-1379

"True knowledge" – the three kinds of true knowledge – knowledge of the recollection of past lives is true knowledge, knowledge of the passing away and reappearance of beings is true knowledge, knowledge of the destruction of the taints is true knowledge. ^1-1380

"Liberation" – the two kinds of liberation – the release of the mind [variant: cittassa ca (Sī., Syā.)], and Nibbāna. ^1-1381

"Knowledge of destruction" – the knowledge of one who possesses the path. ^1-1382

"Knowledge of non-arising" – the knowledge of one who possesses the fruit.
The chapter of summaries is complete. ^1-1383

### The Chapter of Explanations ^1-4-0

#### Explanation of the Sets of Three ^1-4-1-0

What are wholesome states? The wholesome mind of the four planes – these are wholesome states. ^1-1384

What are unwholesome states? The twelve arisings of unwholesome mind – these are unwholesome states. ^1-1385

What are indeterminate states? The results of the four planes, the indeterminate functional mind of the three planes, material form and Nibbāna – these are indeterminate states. ^1-1386

What are states associated with pleasant feeling? The four arisings of mind accompanied by joy from the wholesome mind of the sense sphere, four from the unwholesome mind, five from the results of the wholesome mind of the sense sphere and from the functional mind, the three absorptions of the fine-material sphere in the fourfold method and the four in the fivefold method (wholesome, resultant and functional), the three world-transcending absorptions in the fourfold method and the four in the fivefold method (wholesome and resultant), except the pleasant feeling arisen here – these are states associated with pleasant feeling. ^1-1387

What are states associated with painful feeling? The two arisings of mind accompanied by sadness, body-consciousness accompanied by pain, except the painful feeling arisen here – these are states associated with painful feeling. ^1-1388

What are states associated with feeling that is neither painful nor pleasant? The four arisings of mind accompanied by equanimity from the wholesome mind of the sense sphere, six from the unwholesome mind, ten from the results of the wholesome mind of the sense sphere, six from the results of the unwholesome mind, six from the functional mind, the fourth absorption of the fine-material sphere (wholesome, resultant and functional), the four minds of the immaterial sphere (wholesome, resultant and functional), the fourth world-transcending absorption (wholesome and resultant), except the feeling that is neither painful nor pleasant arisen here – these are states associated with feeling that is neither painful nor pleasant. The three feelings, material form and Nibbāna – these are not to be counted among states associated with pleasant feeling, nor among states associated with painful feeling, nor among states associated with feeling that is neither painful nor pleasant. ^1-1389

What are states that are results? The results of the four planes – these are states that are results. ^1-1390

What are states that produce results? The wholesome mind of the four planes, the unwholesome mind – these are states that produce results. ^1-1391

What are states that neither are results nor produce results? The indeterminate functional mind of the three planes, material form and Nibbāna – these are states that neither are results nor produce results. ^1-1392

What are states produced by past action and subject to clinging? The results of the three planes and whatever material form is due to past action – these are states produced by past action and subject to clinging. ^1-1393

What are states not produced by past action but subject to clinging? The wholesome mind of the three planes, the unwholesome mind, the indeterminate functional mind of the three planes and whatever material form is not due to past action – these are states not produced by past action but subject to clinging. ^1-1394

What are states neither produced by past action nor subject to clinging? The four paths not included in the spheres of existence, the four fruits of the contemplative life and Nibbāna – these are states neither produced by past action nor subject to clinging. ^1-1395

What are states that are defiled and subject to defilement? The twelve arisings of unwholesome mind – these are states that are defiled and subject to defilement. ^1-1396

What are states that are undefiled but subject to defilement? The wholesome mind of the three planes, the results of the three planes, the indeterminate functional mind of the three planes and all material form – these are states that are undefiled but subject to defilement. ^1-1397

What are states that are undefiled and not subject to defilement? The four paths not included in the spheres of existence, the four fruits of the contemplative life and Nibbāna – these are states that are undefiled and not subject to defilement. ^1-1398

What are states with applied thought and sustained thought? The wholesome mind of the sense sphere, the unwholesome mind, eleven arisings of mind from the results of the wholesome mind of the sense sphere, two from the results of the unwholesome mind, eleven from the functional mind, the first absorption of the fine-material sphere (wholesome, resultant and functional), the first world-transcending absorption (wholesome and resultant), except the applied thought and sustained thought arisen here – these are states with applied thought and sustained thought. ^1-1399

What are states without applied thought but with sustained thought only? The second absorption of the fine-material sphere in the fivefold method (wholesome, resultant and functional), the second world-transcending absorption in the fivefold method (wholesome and resultant), except the sustained thought arisen here, and applied thought – these are states without applied thought but with sustained thought only. ^1-1400

What are states without applied thought and without sustained thought? The two sets of five kinds of sense consciousness, the three absorptions of the fine-material sphere in the fourfold method and the three in the fivefold method (wholesome, resultant and functional), the four immaterial attainments (wholesome, resultant and functional), the three world-transcending absorptions in the fourfold method and the three in the fivefold method (wholesome and resultant), the sustained thought arisen in the second absorption [variant: dutiyajjhāne (Sī.)] of the fivefold method, material form and Nibbāna – these are states without applied thought and without sustained thought. Sustained thought arisen together with applied thought is not to be counted among states with applied thought and sustained thought, nor among states without applied thought but with sustained thought only, nor among states without applied thought and without sustained thought. ^1-1401

What are states accompanied by rapture? The four arisings of mind accompanied by joy from the wholesome mind of the sense sphere, four from the unwholesome mind, five from the results of the wholesome mind of the sense sphere, five from the functional mind, the two absorptions of the fine-material sphere in the fourfold method and the three in the fivefold method (wholesome, resultant and functional), the two world-transcending absorptions in the fourfold method and the three in the fivefold method (wholesome and resultant), except the rapture arisen here – these are states accompanied by rapture. ^1-1402

What are states accompanied by happiness? The four arisings of mind accompanied by joy from the wholesome mind of the sense sphere, four from the unwholesome mind, six from the results of the wholesome mind of the sense sphere, five from the functional mind, the three absorptions of the fine-material sphere in the fourfold method and the four in the fivefold method (wholesome, resultant and functional), the three world-transcending absorptions in the fourfold method and the four in the fivefold method (wholesome and resultant), except the happiness arisen here – these are states accompanied by happiness. ^1-1403

What are states accompanied by equanimity? The four arisings of mind accompanied by equanimity from the wholesome mind of the sense sphere, six from the unwholesome mind, ten from the results of the wholesome mind of the sense sphere, six from the results of the unwholesome mind, six from the functional mind, the fourth absorption of the fine-material sphere (wholesome, resultant and functional), the four immaterial attainments (wholesome, resultant and functional), the fourth world-transcending absorption (wholesome and resultant), except the equanimity arisen here – these are states accompanied by equanimity. Rapture does not belong to states accompanied by rapture; it belongs to states accompanied by happiness; it does not belong to states accompanied by equanimity. Happiness does not belong to states accompanied by happiness; it sometimes belongs to states accompanied by rapture; it does not belong to states accompanied by equanimity; and sometimes it is not to be counted among states accompanied by rapture. The two arisings of mind accompanied by sadness, body-consciousness accompanied by pain, the feeling that is equanimity, material form and Nibbāna – these are not to be counted among states accompanied by rapture, nor among states accompanied by happiness, nor among states accompanied by equanimity. ^1-1404

What are states to be abandoned by seeing? The four arisings of mind associated with wrong view, the arising of mind accompanied by doubt – these are states to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1405

What are states to be abandoned by development? The arising of mind accompanied by restlessness – these are states to be abandoned by development. The four arisings of mind accompanied by greed and dissociated from wrong view, the two arisings of mind accompanied by sadness – these are sometimes states to be abandoned by seeing, sometimes states to be abandoned by development. ^1-1406

What are states to be abandoned neither by seeing nor by development? The wholesome mind of the four planes, the results of the four planes, the indeterminate functional mind of the three planes, material form and Nibbāna – these are states to be abandoned neither by seeing nor by development. ^1-1407

What are states whose roots are to be abandoned by seeing? The four arisings of mind associated with wrong view, the arising of mind accompanied by doubt, except the delusion arisen here – these are states whose roots are to be abandoned by seeing. ^1-1408

What are states whose roots are to be abandoned by development? The arising of mind accompanied by restlessness, except the delusion arisen here – these are states whose roots are to be abandoned by development. The four arisings of mind accompanied by greed and dissociated from wrong view, the two arisings of mind accompanied by sadness – these are sometimes states whose roots are to be abandoned by seeing, sometimes states whose roots are to be abandoned by development. ^1-1409

What are states whose roots are to be abandoned neither by seeing nor by development? The delusion accompanied by doubt, the delusion accompanied by restlessness, the wholesome mind of the four planes, the results of the four planes, the indeterminate functional mind of the three planes, material form and Nibbāna – these are states whose roots are to be abandoned neither by seeing nor by development. ^1-1410

What are states leading to accumulation? The wholesome mind of the three planes, the unwholesome mind – these are states leading to accumulation. ^1-1411

What are states leading away from accumulation? The four paths not included in the spheres of existence – these are states leading away from accumulation. ^1-1412

What are states leading neither to nor away from accumulation? The results of the four planes, the indeterminate functional mind of the three planes, material form and Nibbāna – these are states leading neither to nor away from accumulation. ^1-1413

What are states of one in training? The four paths not included in the spheres of existence, and the three lower fruits of the contemplative life – these are states of one in training. ^1-1414

What are states of one beyond training? The highest fruit, the fruit of arahantship – these are states of one beyond training. ^1-1415

What are states of one neither in training nor beyond training? The wholesome mind of the three planes, the unwholesome mind, the results of the three planes, the indeterminate functional mind of the three planes, material form and Nibbāna – these are states of one neither in training nor beyond training. ^1-1416

What are limited states? The wholesome mind of the sense sphere, the unwholesome mind, all the results of the sense sphere, the indeterminate functional mind of the sense sphere and all material form – these are limited states. ^1-1417

What are exalted states? States of the fine-material sphere, states of the immaterial sphere, wholesome and indeterminate – these are exalted states. ^1-1418

What are immeasurable states? The four paths not included in the spheres of existence, the four fruits of the contemplative life and Nibbāna – these are immeasurable states. ^1-1419

What are states with a limited object? All the results of the sense sphere, the functional mind element, the functional mind-consciousness element without roots and accompanied by joy – these are states with a limited object. ^1-1420

What are states with an exalted object? The base of infinite consciousness, the base of neither perception nor non-perception – these are states with an exalted object. ^1-1421
